\section{Notation and reproducibility}\label{nee:aa:app:notation}

\begin{longtable}{L{0.22\textwidth}L{0.69\textwidth}}
\toprule
Notation & Meaning \\
\midrule
\endhead
$A$, $\kappa$ & External atom set and infinite kernel cutoff, with $|A|\geq\kappa$. \\
$\kerat(x)$ & Atoms in $\TC(\{x\})$; the actual transitive-closure kernel. \\
$M_\kappa(A)$ & All objects with fewer than $\kappa$ atoms in their kernel. \\
$\mathcal Z$ & Atom permutations commuting with every named injection. \\
$\Sat(B)$ & Union of signed-path components meeting $B$. \\
$D^{\mathrm{pt}}_\kappa$ & Atoms in $\mathcal Z$-orbits of size below $\kappa$. \\
$D_\kappa$ & Union of entire unpointed type blocks of size below $\kappa$. \\
$\mu_t$, $\delta_t$ & Number of copies of type $t$; cardinality of its local automorphism group. \\
$M$, $P$, $G$ & Presented commutative monoid; its cancellative image; its group completion. \\
$U$, $Q$, $d$ & Units $P\cap(-P)$; quotient $G/U$; its free rank. \\
$\tau(M)$ & Number of connected labelled action types. \\
$g_0$ & Greatest common divisor of the exponents in $f^m=g^n$. \\
$e$ & Order of an infinite component's torsion localization stabilizer. \\
$\delta$ & Repetition count of its boundary word; its local symmetry order. \\
$\Neck(a,b)$ & Rotation-class count of weak compositions of $b$ into $a$ parts. \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\srcs{source~03, Appendix~A (its $\operatorname{ak}$ and $\mathcal N(a,b)$ printed $\kerat$ and $\Neck$). The full notation of this Part is in Section~\ref{nee:aa:sec:notation}.}

To rebuild the article, run \texttt{latexmk -pdf atom\_actions.tex} from the
package directory. The vector figure is included, so compiling the PDF does
not require Python. To reproduce the exact checks, run
\texttt{python3 verify\_presentations.py}; the dependency is SymPy.
The figure can be regenerated with
\texttt{python3 figures/make\_figure.py} using Matplotlib.
The accompanying \texttt{README.txt} lists the commands and the precise
verification scope. No external dataset or network request is needed to run
the finite checks. (Source~03; in ProveIt the delivered article and \texttt{README.txt} are not shipped, the programs have prefixed names and write beside themselves by default, and the report's README gives commands that do not overwrite the shipped records: the note in Section~\ref{nee:aa:sec:verification}.)

\section{Source audit and limits of the conclusion}\label{nee:aa:app:audit}

The research-interest connection was checked against Glazer's thesis and
Glazer--Yao's primary paper. The repository questions were read directly in
the pinned current source, and the related bounded-kernel report was checked
to avoid re-presenting its finite-orbit Replacement theorem as new.
Literature searches also covered localization of monoid acts, torsion-free
indecomposable acts, affine-monoid units and cones, and the rational Catalan
special case. The standard sources listed below support those ingredients.

No inspected source was found stating the exact general type-count
trichotomy in \cref{nee:aa:thm:types}. This is a limited search result, not evidence
that every relevant publication has been excluded. The strongest supported
claim is that the article proves its stated results and answers the identified
repository questions under explicit conventions. Questions about unrestricted
choiceless set theory, noninitial elementary embeddings, arbitrary
noninjective operations, and field-wide historical priority are outside that
claim. (Source~03, Appendix~B.)

\begin{writenote}[The cited papers, checked at the write, 4 October 2026, batch~96]
Source~03's ``answers the identified repository questions'' is re-scoped in Section~\ref{nee:aa:sec:questions-answered}: Question~23.3 is answered, Questions~23.1 and~23.2 with pure real parameters. The arXiv records of~\cite{GY2026} and~\cite{Yao2023} were checked at the write of Part~\ref{nee:part:one} (Section~\ref{nee:sec:provenance}); the new citations were not rechecked at this write, and the page and item references in them (Bailey--Renshaw's Lemmas~6.4--6.8, Boardman's Theorems~7--8, Koch's Lemmas~3.4.4--3.4.5, Glazer--Yao's Definition~18 and Theorem~19) are the sources' own.
\end{writenote}
